ambiguity.codon_set: 3/5
ambiguity.codon_set/01 · correct · 37.5s · $0.00211

Exact prompt

## Task

List every amino acid or stop that the selected IUPAC codon could encode under NCBI genetic code 11.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "GTGGTCTGCTTGATTYTNCGGTTAATTGGTTAG",
  "codon_index": 6
}
```

## Rules

- Inspect only the codon at `codon_index`. Consider every concrete codon allowed by its IUPAC symbols, expanding the three positions independently.
- Use ordinary internal-codon meanings, not an initiation override.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- Return a list of double-quoted one-letter amino-acid symbols; represent a stop by "STOP".
- Include each possible internal-translation outcome once, sorted in ASCII order, including "STOP".

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

Expected answer

["F","L"]

Final prediction

["F", "L"]

Exact prompt

## Task

Choose the smallest set of candidate RNA probes whose match patterns distinguish every CDS in the panel.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds_panel": [
    "GTGCCGATGGGTTACGCCTTTCCCAAGGCCTAA",
    "GTGCCGATGGGTTACGCCTTCCCCAAGGCCTAA",
    "GTGCCTATGGGTTACGCCTTTCCCAAGGCCTAA"
  ],
  "candidate_probes": {
    "probe_00": "ACCCAUCGGCAC",
    "probe_01": "GUAACCCAUCGG",
    "probe_02": "GGCGUAACCCAU",
    "probe_03": "AAAGGCGUAACC",
    "probe_04": "GGGAAAGGCGUA",
    "probe_05": "CUUGGGAAAGGC",
    "probe_06": "GGCCUUGGGAAA",
    "probe_07": "UUAGGCCUUGGG"
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Transcribe each CDS to coding RNA and use only the supplied candidate probes.
- A probe hits an RNA if at least one equal-length window is its exact reverse complement.
- For each RNA, record one hit/no-hit bit per selected probe in the selected probe order. Every input RNA must have a different bit string.

## Selection

Minimize the number of selected probes, then choose the lexicographically smallest sorted list of probe IDs. Compare complete ID strings in ASCII order.

## Reference material

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### RNA reverse complement

RNA complementary bases are A↔U and C↔G. Complement each base, then reverse the sequence to write its complementary partner 5′→3′.

## Answer

- Return a list of double-quoted probe IDs, spelled exactly as supplied, unique and sorted in ASCII order.
- A valid zero-probe panel is [].

If no valid result exists, return:

```text
IMPOSSIBLE
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

Expected answer

## Task

Join CDS A, the linker, and CDS B into a single CDS under the assembly rule below.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds_a": "ATGGAGGTGAAGGAGATCCTCCTCAGGGCTTAG",
  "cds_b": "ATGTGGAAGGCGGATCGCTAG",
  "linker_cds": "GGCGGTAGC"
}
```

## Rules

- Remove only the terminal stop codon from A and the first codon from B.
- Concatenate the remaining A sequence, `linker_cds`, and the remaining B sequence in that order.
- Keep A’s first codon and B’s terminal stop. Do not remove or alter any other bases.

### Codon length

A codon is a group of three consecutive nucleotides.

## Task

## Inputs

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGAAAAAAGGTAAAATTGATATTGTTGATGCTTCATAA",
  "residue_index": 7,
  "target_aa": "H"
}
```

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Only the selected internal codon may change. Preserve every other DNA base.

## Selection

Minimize the total number of changed nucleotides permitted by this task; among equally minimal candidates, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string (A<C<G<T).

## Reference material

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.

If no valid result exists, return:

```text
IMPOSSIBLE
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

## Task

Remove every occurrence of the forbidden peptide using only the allowed amino-acid substitutions.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

Numeric keys in position maps are 1-based positions in the complete protein or its aligned codon sequence, including the initiator as position 1.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGTCGGGGCGGCGCAACGGCTGA",
  "forbidden_peptide": "RR",
  "allowed_residues": {
    "5": [
      "R",
      "K"
    ]
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Remove every literal occurrence of the forbidden peptide.
- Only the listed amino-acid positions may change, within their allowed residue sets. All other residues and both terminal codons stay fixed.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Numeric keys in position maps are 1-based positions in the complete protein or its aligned codon sequence, including the initiator as position 1.

```json

  "forbidden_peptide": "MM",
  "allowed_residues": {

      "M",
      "C"
    ]
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Remove every literal occurrence of the forbidden peptide.
- Only the listed amino-acid positions may change, within their allowed residue sets. All other residues and both terminal codons stay fixed.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Task

List the protein fragments produced by complete cleavage under the benchmark TEV rule.

## Inputs

- Translate the CDS using NCBI genetic code 11, stopping at the first in-frame stop.
- For this benchmark, recognize only the exact sequence `ENLYFQG` and cut it as `ENLYFQ|G`. Keep G on the downstream fragment.
- Cut every occurrence and return the nonempty fragments in their original protein order.

## Task

Rewrite the CDS synonymously using the allowed codons at each internal position.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

Numeric keys in position maps are 1-based positions in the complete protein or its aligned codon sequence, including the initiator as position 1.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGGACTTCTTCGGGCTGCGGTGA",
  "allowed_codons": {
    "2": [
      "GAT"
    ],
    "3": [
      "TTC"
    ],
    "4": [
      "TTT"
    ],
    "5": [
      "GGG"
    ],
    "6": [
      "CTC"
    ],
    "7": [
      "CGG"
    ]
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Only internal sense codons may change. For each such position, choose a synonymous codon from its `allowed_codons` entry.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.

## Task

Rewrite the CDS synonymously using the allowed codons at each internal position.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

Numeric keys in position maps are 1-based positions in the complete protein or its aligned codon sequence, including the initiator as position 1.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGGAATTATGTCGTGCCCGCTAA",
  "allowed_codons": {
    "2": [
      "GAG"
    ],
    "3": [
      "CTA"
    ],
    "4": [
      "TGT"
    ],
    "5": [
      "CGA"
    ],
    "6": [
      "GCA"
    ],
    "7": [
      "AGG"
    ]
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Only internal sense codons may change. For each such position, choose a synonymous codon from its `allowed_codons` entry.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
```

## Task

Use permitted synonymous DNA substitutions to make every listed RNA pair a canonical Watson–Crick pair.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGTTCGGTTCCGGTGATAATTAA",
  "pairs": [
    [
      2,
      19
    ],
    [
      3,
      18
    ],
    [
      4,
      17
    ]
  ],
  "editable_positions": [
    18
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Change DNA bases only at `editable_positions`.
- In the candidate coding RNA, every position pair listed in `pairs` must be a canonical Watson–Crick pair. Exclude wobble.
- Check the supplied pairs only; no folding prediction is required.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### Canonical RNA base pairs

The canonical Watson–Crick RNA pairs are A–U, U–A, C–G, and G–C.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### Canonical RNA base pairs

The canonical Watson–Crick RNA pairs are A–U, U–A, C–G, and G–C.

```json
    ]
  ],
```

```json
    "UA": 2,
    "CG": 3,
    "GC": 3
  },
  "direction": "minimize"
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Use the supplied pair map unchanged. Every listed pair must be canonical in the candidate coding RNA.
- The score is the sum of the `pair_scores` entries for the bases at each listed pair.
- For `direction=maximize`, choose the greatest total; for `minimize`, choose the least.

- Use the supplied pair map unchanged. Every listed pair must be canonical in the candidate coding RNA.
- The score is the sum of the `pair_scores` entries for the bases at each listed pair.
- For `direction=maximize`, choose the greatest total; for `minimize`, choose the least.

### Synonymous codons

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

## Task

Rewrite the CDS synonymously while keeping the number of changed DNA bases within `edit_bounds`.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGGAACGAAATTTTATAAACTGA",
  "edit_bounds": [
    1,
    3
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Count the nucleotide positions where the rewritten CDS differs from the original. This count must lie between both inclusive `edit_bounds`.
- Use substitutions only, with no insertions or deletions.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
```

## Task

Create a panel of distinct synonymous CDSs with the requested RNA CG counts and a shared DNA GC count.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCTCAGTTCTCATAATGCCTAA",
  "target_cg_counts": [
    0,
    1
  ],
  "gc_count": 11
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Every returned CDS must be different from every other panel member.
- Keep the same order as `target_cg_counts`: the first member must meet the first requested count, the second member the second count, and so on.
- Count overlapping CG occurrences in each member’s coding RNA, including occurrences at codon boundaries.
- Each complete DNA sequence must contain exactly `gc_count` G/C bases.

## Selection

Among complete feasible panels, choose the lexicographically smallest list of DNA strings: compare the first differing member using A<C<G<T. Resolve choices across the whole panel, including distinctness; do not choose each member independently.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

## Answer

- Return a list of double-quoted, continuous uppercase synonymous DNA sequences.
- Entry i must meet target_cg_counts[i], in that input order. All members must be distinct.

If no valid result exists, return:

```text
IMPOSSIBLE
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

## Task

Find every occurrence of the IUPAC DNA pattern on the strand or strands specified by `strand_mode`.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGTGCACGCTGATCGCCGAGGCGCAGGCCCATGAGGTGCGCGCGCTGTGA",
  "pattern": "GCC",
  "strand_mode": "both"
}
```

## Rules

- Include overlapping matches.
- With `strand_mode=forward`, search only the supplied coding strand. With `strand_mode=both`, also search for the reverse complement of the pattern.
- Express every hit as an interval on the original forward CDS. A minus-strand hit still has its smaller forward coordinate first.
- If the pattern equals its own reverse complement, report each matching interval once, with strand `+`.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

### DNA reverse complement

DNA complementary bases are A↔T and C↔G. Complement each base, then reverse the sequence to write the opposite strand 5′→3′.

## Answer

- `start` and `end` are 1-based inclusive coordinates on the original forward CDS. `strand` is "+" or "-".
- Include overlapping hits and sort by start, then end, then strand (+ before -). For a reverse-complement-palindromic pattern, report each interval once with strand "+". An empty result is [].

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
[
  {
    "start": <positive integer>,
    "end": <positive integer>,
    "strand": <"+" or "-">
  }
]
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Begin the raw JSON on the next line; it may span multiple lines and ends your response.

Expected answer

[{"end":18,"start":16,"strand":"+"},{"end":23,"start":21,"strand":"-"},{"end":29,"start":27,"strand":"-"},{"end":30,"start":28,"strand":"+"}]

Final prediction

```json
[
  {"start": 16, "end": 18, "strand": "+"},
  {"start": 21, "end": 23, "strand": "-"},
  {"start": 22, "end": 24, "strand": "+"}
]
```

```json
  "cds": "ATGGACATTCCGCAGGCCGATTATGCCAAAGATGAAAGAGTGGAGTTTACGGCACGTATCGAATATTAA",
  "pattern": "ACT",
  "strand_mode": "both"
}
```

## Rules

- Include overlapping matches.
- With `strand_mode=forward`, search only the supplied coding strand. With `strand_mode=both`, also search for the reverse complement of the pattern.
- Express every hit as an interval on the original forward CDS. A minus-strand hit still has its smaller forward coordinate first.
- If the pattern equals its own reverse complement, report each matching interval once, with strand `+`.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

### DNA reverse complement

DNA complementary bases are A↔T and C↔G. Complement each base, then reverse the sequence to write the opposite strand 5′→3′.

## Answer

- `start` and `end` are 1-based inclusive coordinates on the original forward CDS. `strand` is "+" or "-".
- Include overlapping hits and sort by start, then end, then strand (+ before -). For a reverse-complement-palindromic pattern, report each interval once with strand "+". An empty result is [].

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

```text
    "start": <positive integer>,
    "end": <positive integer>,
    "strand": <"+" or "-">
  }
]
```

joint.clone_ready_cds/06 · token_cap · 146.0s · $0.00662

Exact prompt

## Task

Find one synonymous CDS that removes every forbidden DNA pattern and satisfies every GC-window bound.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

GC bounds are inclusive fractions. A string such as `"1/3"` denotes that exact rational value.

```json
{
  "cds": "GTGCTCGAATTCTTCGGCAACTGA",
  "forbidden_patterns": [
    "GAATTC"
  ],
  "strand_mode": "both",
  "window": 9,
  "gc_bounds": [
    "1/9",
    "5/9"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Remove every forbidden IUPAC pattern hit under `strand_mode`, including overlapping hits and hits spanning codons.
- Move a window of length `window` over the full final CDS one base at a time. Every full window must satisfy the inclusive GC-fraction bounds.
- Check the motif and GC conditions together on the same final sequence.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

### DNA reverse complement

DNA complementary bases are A↔T and C↔G. Complement each base, then reverse the sequence to write the opposite strand 5′→3′.

### GC fraction

GC fraction is the number of G and C bases divided by the number of bases in the sequence being measured.

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.

If no valid result exists, return:

```text
IMPOSSIBLE
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

Expected answer

GTGCTAGAATTTTTCGGAAACTGA

Final prediction

Generated-token budget exhausted

## Task

## Inputs

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCTGGAATTCTTCATCAACTAA",
  "forbidden_patterns": [
    "GAATTC"
  ],
  "strand_mode": "both",
  "window": 9,
  "gc_bounds": [
    "1/9",
    "3/9"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Remove every forbidden IUPAC pattern hit under `strand_mode`, including overlapping hits and hits spanning codons.
- Move a window of length `window` over the full final CDS one base at a time. Every full window must satisfy the inclusive GC-fraction bounds.
- Check the motif and GC conditions together on the same final sequence.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

## Task

Find a synonymous CDS within the edit budget that meets the RNA pair-score threshold and has the highest permitted CAI.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGAAAGGAATAGTGCCCGGCTAA",
  "pairs": [
    [
      11,
      23
    ],
    [
      12,
      22
    ],
    [
      13,
      21
    ]
  ],
  "pair_scores": {
    "AU": 2,
    "UA": 2,
    "CG": 3,
    "GC": 3
  },
  "edit_bounds": [
    1,
    3
  ],
  "min_pair_score": 6,
  "weights": {
    "GGA": 0.8,
    "GGC": 1.0,
    "GGG": 0.6,
    "GGT": 0.2,
    "ATA": 0.4,
    "ATC": 0.6,
    "ATT": 1.0,
    "AAA": 0.6,
    "AAG": 1.0,
    "CCA": 0.4,
    "CCC": 0.6,
    "CCG": 1.0,
    "CCT": 0.2,
    "GTA": 0.8,
    "GTC": 0.8,
    "GTG": 1.0,
    "GTT": 0.2
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 for codon interpretation.
- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Count changed nucleotide positions relative to the original CDS. The count must meet both inclusive `edit_bounds`.
- Keep the supplied RNA pair map fixed. Every listed pair must be canonical, and the sum of its supplied pair scores must be at least `min_pair_score`.
- Among candidates meeting all constraints, maximize CAI using `weights`, excluding the initiator and terminal stop.

## Selection

After achieving the stated optimum, break ties by the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, with A<C<G<T. Compare objective values before rounding.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### Canonical RNA base pairs

The canonical Watson–Crick RNA pairs are A–U, U–A, C–G, and G–C.

### Codon adaptation index

For n included codons with supplied positive weights w₁,…,wₙ, CAI = (w₁ × … × wₙ)^(1/n), the geometric mean of those weights.

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.

## Task

Design an IUPAC codon that covers every target amino acid and cannot encode a stop.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGTATGCAATGACGCTCTACGACAACAGCATCGGCGCGCTCATGCAGGGACAGATCCTTTGA",
  "codon_index": 3,
  "target_residues": [
    "K",
    "R"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Choose one three-symbol IUPAC DNA codon for the selected internal position.
- Its concrete expansions must encode every residue in `target_residues` and must never encode a stop under NCBI genetic code 11.
- Use the ranking below to choose among qualifying IUPAC codons.

## Selection

Require target-residue coverage and no stops. Minimize extra amino-acid types, then the number of concrete codons, then the IUPAC triplet in ASCII order A<B<C<D<G<H<K<M<N<R<S<T<V<W<Y. Compare the symbols themselves, not their expanded nucleotide sets.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

## Task

Design an IUPAC codon that covers every target amino acid and cannot encode a stop.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGGTGCCCGGGGTGCCGAACGGAGCAGCATAA",
  "codon_index": 3,
  "target_residues": [
    "A",
    "G"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Choose one three-symbol IUPAC DNA codon for the selected internal position.
- Its concrete expansions must encode every residue in `target_residues` and must never encode a stop under NCBI genetic code 11.
- Use the ranking below to choose among qualifying IUPAC codons.

## Selection

Require target-residue coverage and no stops. Minimize extra amino-acid types, then the number of concrete codons, then the IUPAC triplet in ASCII order A<B<C<D<G<H<K<M<N<R<S<T<V<W<Y. Compare the symbols themselves, not their expanded nucleotide sets.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

## Task

Report how the supplied DNA replacement changes the peptides encoded by the two annotated reading windows.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

`replacement` is `[first_base, last_base, new_text]` in the original DNA coordinates.

`frames` lists frame A first and frame B second. Each record supplies its inclusive DNA interval and strand.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGATCGTCCTCATGATGGTGTGA",
  "frames": [
    {
      "interval": [
        7,
        12
      ],
      "strand": "+"
    },
    {
      "interval": [
        6,
        11
      ],
      "strand": "+"
    }
  ],
  "replacement": [
    8,
    8,
    "A"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- For each supplied window, extract its full DNA interval and reverse-complement it if its strand is `-`.
- Translate every complete triplet under ordinary internal NCBI genetic code 11 meanings. Do not treat the first triplet as an initiator.
- Keep stop symbols as `*` and continue translating subsequent triplets.
- Apply the equal-length DNA replacement to the original CDS coordinates, then repeat the same window extraction and translation.

## Reference material

### DNA reverse complement

DNA complementary bases are A↔T and C↔G. Complement each base, then reverse the sequence to write the opposite strand 5′→3′.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- Return exactly two records in supplied frame order: "A" for the first annotated window and "B" for the second.
- `original_peptide` and `edited_peptide` are the full internal translations before and after editing, as continuous uppercase strings retaining every stop as *. `changed` is true exactly when the two strings differ.

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
[
  {
    "frame": <"A" or "B">,
    "original_peptide": <amino-acid string, retaining stops as *>,
    "edited_peptide": <amino-acid string, retaining stops as *>,
    "changed": <boolean>
  }
]
```

DNA complementary bases are A↔T and C↔G. Complement each base, then reverse the sequence to write the opposite strand 5′→3′.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S

TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W

CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Task

Change the peptide in annotated frame A to `target_peptide_a` while preserving the peptide in frame B.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

`frames` lists frame A first and frame B second. Each record supplies its inclusive DNA interval and strand.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCTGATCGGGGAACTGCGTTGA",
  "frames": [
    {
      "interval": [
        7,
        12
      ],
      "strand": "+"
    },
    {
      "interval": [
        6,
        11
      ],
      "strand": "+"
    }
  ],
  "target_peptide_a": "TG",
  "editable_positions": [
    8
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Only DNA positions in `editable_positions` may change, using substitutions without changing the sequence length.
- Extract each annotated DNA window, reversing and complementing minus-strand windows. Use ordinary internal NCBI genetic code 11 meanings throughout, without an initiation override.
- Keep stops as `*` and continue past them.
- The final A peptide must equal `target_peptide_a`; the final B peptide must equal its original complete peptide.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### DNA reverse complement

```text
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E

```

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.

## Task

Choose a tryptic peptide that distinguishes the target CDS’s protein from every protein encoded by the comparison panel.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "target_cds": "ATGCGCCTGCCCTTCGCCGAAGAGATCTGGGACGACGAGACGTTCGCCATCGTGTGA",
  "background_cds_panel": [
    "ATGCGCCTGCCCTTCGCCGAAGAGATCTGGGACGACGAGTGTTTCGCCATCGTGTGA"
  ],
  "length_bounds": [
    3,
    18
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Translate every CDS under NCBI genetic code 11, ending each protein at its first in-frame stop.
- Digest every protein completely with trypsin, with zero missed cleavage sites.
- A peptide qualifies only if its sequence occurs in the target digest and in none of the comparison digests. Ignore how many copies occur.
- Its length must satisfy both inclusive `length_bounds`.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W

CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K

GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Trypsin cleavage specificity

## Task

Fuse A–linker–B under the module-join rule, find every peptide-pattern match, and report its residue and fused-DNA intervals.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds_a": "ATGCTGCCCCGTCACTGGCGCTTCGCCCTCAAGGGGTCGCTCTCTCAGACGCACGTCTATTGA",
  "cds_b": "ATGGCTATTCTCGCGCGCTGA",
  "linker_cds": "GGCGGCTCG",
  "pattern": "YGGSA"
}
```

## Rules

- Remove A’s terminal stop and B’s first codon, then concatenate the remaining A DNA, linker, and remaining B DNA. Keep B’s terminal stop.
- Translate the entire fused CDS with NCBI genetic code 11, ending at its first in-frame stop.
- Find every overlapping peptide-pattern match, including matches spanning the assembly junction.
- Pattern syntax: a letter matches itself; `X` matches any residue; `[set]` matches a listed residue; `{set}` excludes listed residues.
- Report DNA intervals in the assembled CDS’s coordinates, not in either source CDS’s coordinates.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- `fused_cds` is the exact assembled continuous uppercase DNA sequence.
- Each match gives aa_interval=[first_residue, last_residue] in the fused protein and nt_interval=[first_base, last_base] in fused_cds, both with 1-based inclusive integer endpoints.
- Include all overlapping matches, sorted by protein start and then end. An empty match list is [].

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "fused_cds": <DNA string>,
  "matches": [
    {
      "aa_interval": [<first residue>, <last residue>],
      "nt_interval": [<first base>, <last base>]
    }
  ]
}
```

## Task

Design a stop-free IUPAC codon covering every target amino acid, then report how many concrete codons encode each amino acid in its expansion.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCGTGACCACAACCTGGAAGGCAAGGGCCGCTGA",
  "codon_index": 3,
  "target_residues": [
    "A",
    "G",
    "V"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Choose a single IUPAC triplet for the selected internal codon. Its expansions must cover all `target_residues` and contain no NCBI genetic code 11 stop.
- Expand that same selected triplet into distinct concrete DNA codons, translate each with its ordinary internal meaning, and count how many encode each amino acid.

## Selection

Require target-residue coverage and no stops. Minimize extra amino-acid types, then the number of concrete codons, then the IUPAC triplet in ASCII order A<B<C<D<G<H<K<M<N<R<S<T<V<W<Y. Compare the symbols themselves, not their expanded nucleotide sets.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- `codon` is the selected uppercase IUPAC DNA triplet. `residue_counts` maps every produced one-letter amino-acid symbol to the number of distinct concrete codons encoding it in that triplet’s expansion.
- Include all positive counts, including amino acids outside target_residues. Their sum is the expansion size. These are codon multiplicities rather than probabilities or full-protein residue counts. A valid design is stop-free.

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "status": "ok",
  "codon": <IUPAC DNA triplet>,
  "residue_counts": {"<one-letter amino-acid symbol>": <positive integer count>}
}
```

If no valid result exists, return:

```json
{
  "status": "impossible",
  "codon": null,
  "residue_counts": null
}
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Begin the raw JSON on the next line; it may span multiple lines and ends your response.

Expected answer

{"codon":"GBA","residue_counts":{"A":1,"G":1,"V":1},"status":"ok"}

Final prediction

{
  "status": "ok",
  "codon": "GBC",
  "residue_counts": {"A": 1, "G": 1, "V": 1}
}

Design a stop-free IUPAC codon covering every target amino acid, then report how many concrete codons encode each amino acid in its expansion.

## Inputs

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGTTGCATAATGGCATGGACGGCGGTATGAGTATTCCTTTCGATATAGATTTGACTTGGATGTAA",
  "codon_index": 3,
  "target_residues": [
    "N",
    "Q"
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Choose a single IUPAC triplet for the selected internal codon. Its expansions must cover all `target_residues` and contain no NCBI genetic code 11 stop.
- Expand that same selected triplet into distinct concrete DNA codons, translate each with its ordinary internal meaning, and count how many encode each amino acid.

## Selection

Require target-residue coverage and no stops. Minimize extra amino-acid types, then the number of concrete codons, then the IUPAC triplet in ASCII order A<B<C<D<G<H<K<M<N<R<S<T<V<W<Y. Compare the symbols themselves, not their expanded nucleotide sets.

## Reference material

### IUPAC DNA base sets

An IUPAC symbol permits any base in its listed set. Choices at different positions are independent.

```text
A=A  C=C  G=G  T=T
R=AG  Y=CT  S=CG  W=AT  K=GT  M=AC
B=CGT  D=AGT  H=ACT  V=ACG  N=ACGT
```

```text

TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W

CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T

AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

Exact prompt

## Task

Find every peptide-pattern match encoded by this CDS and return both its residue interval and its encoding DNA interval.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGGGACTCGGCAGTCCGTTCAACGAACTGTTCCGGTGA",
  "pattern": "L{P}[S]"
}
```

## Rules

- Translate with NCBI genetic code 11, stopping at the first in-frame stop.
- Pattern syntax: a letter matches itself; `X` matches any residue; `[set]` matches a listed residue; `{set}` excludes listed residues.
- Include overlapping peptide-pattern matches.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Answer

- `aa_interval` is [first_residue, last_residue] in the translated protein. `nt_interval` is [first_base, last_base] in the original CDS. Both have 1-based inclusive integer endpoints.

```json
  "ph": 8.0,
  "pka": {
    "N_term": 8.0,
    "C_term": 4.0,
    "K": 10.5,
    "R": 12.5,
    "H": 6.0,
    "D": 3.9,
    "E": 4.1,
    "C": 8.3,
    "Y": 10.1
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Translate once with NCBI genetic code 11, stopping at the first in-frame stop. Use that same complete protein for every measurement.
- Use an unmodified linear protein with both termini free. Do not remove the initiator or form disulfides.
- Use the independent-site Henderson–Hasselbalch calculation with the supplied `pka` values. Search pH 0–14 for pI.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Monoisotopic residue masses

Use the following incorporated residue masses in Da. A free linear peptide has the sum of its residue masses plus one H₂O, 18.010565 Da.

```text
A=71.037114  C=103.009185  D=115.026943  E=129.042593
F=147.068414  G=57.021464  H=137.058912  I=113.084064
K=128.094963  L=113.084064  M=131.040485  N=114.042927
P=97.052764  Q=128.058578  R=156.101111  S=87.032028
T=101.047679  V=99.068414  W=186.079313  Y=163.063329
```

### Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy scores

Use these Kyte–Doolittle residue scores. Whole-protein mean hydropathy is their arithmetic mean, counting every residue occurrence.

```text
A=1.8  R=-4.5  N=-3.5  D=-3.5
C=2.5  Q=-3.5  E=-3.5  G=-0.4
H=-3.2  I=4.5  L=3.8  K=-3.9
M=1.9  F=2.8  P=-1.6  S=-0.8
T=-0.7  W=-0.9  Y=-1.3  V=4.2
```

### Ionization and net charge

A basic site contributes +1/(1+10^(pH−pKa)); an acidic site contributes −1/(1+10^(pKa−pH)).

Basic sites: the N terminus and K, R, H side chains. Acidic sites: the C terminus and D, E, C, Y side chains. Count each terminus once and each listed side chain once per residue occurrence; sum their contributions.

## Answer

- `residue_counts` maps each observed one-letter amino-acid symbol to its positive integer count; omit absent residues.
- `mass_da` is neutral monoisotopic mass including one water. `mean_hydropathy` is the mean Kyte–Doolittle score.
- `charge` is net charge at the supplied ph. `pi` is the zero-charge pH using the supplied pka values.
- Use JSON numbers rounded once: mass_da, mean_hydropathy, and charge to 4 decimal places; pi to 2. Use the component tasks’ constants and rounding rules.

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "residue_counts": {"<one-letter amino-acid symbol>": <positive integer count>},
  "mass_da": <number>,
  "mean_hydropathy": <number>,
  "charge": <number>,
  "pi": <number>
}
```


### Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy scores

Use these Kyte–Doolittle residue scores. Whole-protein mean hydropathy is their arithmetic mean, counting every residue occurrence.

```text
A=1.8  R=-4.5  N=-3.5  D=-3.5
C=2.5  Q=-3.5  E=-3.5  G=-0.4
H=-3.2  I=4.5  L=3.8  K=-3.9
M=1.9  F=2.8  P=-1.6  S=-0.8
T=-0.7  W=-0.9  Y=-1.3  V=4.2
```

### Ionization and net charge

A basic site contributes +1/(1+10^(pH−pKa)); an acidic site contributes −1/(1+10^(pKa−pH)).

Basic sites: the N terminus and K, R, H side chains. Acidic sites: the C terminus and D, E, C, Y side chains. Count each terminus once and each listed side chain once per residue occurrence; sum their contributions.

## Answer

- `residue_counts` maps each observed one-letter amino-acid symbol to its positive integer count; omit absent residues.
- `mass_da` is neutral monoisotopic mass including one water. `mean_hydropathy` is the mean Kyte–Doolittle score.
- `charge` is net charge at the supplied ph. `pi` is the zero-charge pH using the supplied pka values.
- Use JSON numbers rounded once: mass_da, mean_hydropathy, and charge to 4 decimal places; pi to 2. Use the component tasks’ constants and rounding rules.

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

## Task

Translate the CDS and report residue counts, neutral monoisotopic mass, mean Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy, charge at ph, and pI under pka.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCACAAGGTCGCGCTCGAGGAGCGCGCCATCCTGAAGCAGTGA",
  "ph": 7.0,
  "pka": {
    "N_term": 8.0,
    "C_term": 4.0,
    "K": 10.5,
    "R": 12.5,
    "H": 6.0,
    "D": 3.9,
    "E": 4.1,
    "C": 8.3,
    "Y": 10.1
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Translate once with NCBI genetic code 11, stopping at the first in-frame stop. Use that same complete protein for every measurement.
- Use an unmodified linear protein with both termini free. Do not remove the initiator or form disulfides.
- Use the independent-site Henderson–Hasselbalch calculation with the supplied `pka` values. Search pH 0–14 for pI.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

```text
P=97.052764  Q=128.058578  R=156.101111  S=87.032028

```

- Use an unmodified linear protein with both termini free. Do not remove the initiator or form disulfides.
- Use the independent-site Henderson–Hasselbalch calculation with the supplied `pka` values. Search pH 0–14 for pI.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Monoisotopic residue masses

Use the following incorporated residue masses in Da. A free linear peptide has the sum of its residue masses plus one H₂O, 18.010565 Da.

```text
A=71.037114  C=103.009185  D=115.026943  E=129.042593
F=147.068414  G=57.021464  H=137.058912  I=113.084064
K=128.094963  L=113.084064  M=131.040485  N=114.042927
P=97.052764  Q=128.058578  R=156.101111  S=87.032028
T=101.047679  V=99.068414  W=186.079313  Y=163.063329
```

### Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy scores

Use these Kyte–Doolittle residue scores. Whole-protein mean hydropathy is their arithmetic mean, counting every residue occurrence.

```text
A=1.8  R=-4.5  N=-3.5  D=-3.5
C=2.5  Q=-3.5  E=-3.5  G=-0.4
H=-3.2  I=4.5  L=3.8  K=-3.9
M=1.9  F=2.8  P=-1.6  S=-0.8
T=-0.7  W=-0.9  Y=-1.3  V=4.2
```

### Ionization and net charge

A basic site contributes +1/(1+10^(pH−pKa)); an acidic site contributes −1/(1+10^(pKa−pH)).

Basic sites: the N terminus and K, R, H side chains. Acidic sites: the C terminus and D, E, C, Y side chains. Count each terminus once and each listed side chain once per residue occurrence; sum their contributions.

## Answer

- `residue_counts` maps each observed one-letter amino-acid symbol to its positive integer count; omit absent residues.
- `mass_da` is neutral monoisotopic mass including one water. `mean_hydropathy` is the mean Kyte–Doolittle score.
- `charge` is net charge at the supplied ph. `pi` is the zero-charge pH using the supplied pka values.
- Use JSON numbers rounded once: mass_da, mean_hydropathy, and charge to 4 decimal places; pi to 2. Use the component tasks’ constants and rounding rules.

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "residue_counts": {"<one-letter amino-acid symbol>": <positive integer count>},
  "mass_da": <number>,
  "mean_hydropathy": <number>,
  "charge": <number>,
  "pi": <number>
}
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Begin the raw JSON on the next line; it may span multiple lines and ends your response.

Expected answer

{"charge":0.163,"mass_da":1344.6054,"mean_hydropathy":-0.0167,"pi":6.04,"residue_counts":{"A":1,"C":1,"D":1,"F":1,"I":1,"K":1,"M":1,"N":1,"T":4}}

Final prediction

```json
{
  "residue_counts": {
    "M": 1,
    "I": 1,
    "A": 1,
    "D": 1,
    "T": 3,
    "R": 1,
    "N": 2,
    "L": 1,
    "F": 1
  },

  "charge": 0.1634,
  "pi": 6.13
}
```

## Task

Repair the listed RNA pairs through permitted DNA substitutions, and verify that the final CDS still encodes the original protein.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGACCGCCCACGCCGAGGTATGA",
  "pairs": [
    [
      3,
      21
    ],
    [
      4,
      20
    ],
    [
      5,
      19
    ]
  ],
  "editable_positions": [
    21
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Only DNA positions in `editable_positions` may change.
- Every listed pair must be canonical in the final coding RNA, with no wobble pairing. Do not infer unlisted pairs.
- Verify that this same final DNA still encodes the complete original protein under NCBI genetic code 11.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### Canonical RNA base pairs

The canonical Watson–Crick RNA pairs are A–U, U–A, C–G, and G–C.

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.


## Task

Repair the listed RNA pairs through permitted DNA substitutions, and verify that the final CDS still encodes the original protein.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGATGACCTTCATTCCCGCATAA",
  "pairs": [
    [
      11,
      24
    ],
    [
      12,
      23
    ],
    [
      13,
      22
    ]
  ],
  "editable_positions": [
    12
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Keep the DNA length, complete encoded protein, original first codon, and terminal stop codon unchanged.
- Only DNA positions in `editable_positions` may change.
- Every listed pair must be canonical in the final coding RNA, with no wobble pairing. Do not infer unlisted pairs.
- Verify that this same final DNA still encodes the complete original protein under NCBI genetic code 11.

## Selection

Among all sequences satisfying every condition, return the lexicographically smallest full DNA string, comparing bases in the order A<C<G<T. This is a tie-break among feasible sequences, not a minimum-edit objective.

## Reference material

### Synonymous codons

Synonymous codons encode the same amino acid. Replacing a codon with a synonymous codon preserves that residue in the protein.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### Canonical RNA base pairs

The canonical Watson–Crick RNA pairs are A–U, U–A, C–G, and G–C.

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase DNA sequence.

If no valid result exists, return:

```text
IMPOSSIBLE
```

ATGATGACCTTTATTCCCGCATAA

Final prediction

ATGATGACCTTTATTCCCGCATAA

## Task

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

In an edit object, `replace` is `[first_base, last_base, new_text]`; an empty `new_text` deletes that interval. `insert_after` gives an original base boundary and `sequence` gives the inserted text. Apply all edits to the original coordinates, simultaneously.

```json
{
  "cds": "TTGGATTACCAAGAAAAAAAAGAAAACCTTGCGTAG",
  "rna_edits": [
    {
      "replace": [
        10,
        10,
        "U"
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Transcribe the CDS and apply the supplied edits using the original RNA coordinates.
- Translate the edited RNA from its first base with NCBI genetic code 11. Do not search for a new start position after an edit.
- Stop at the first in-frame stop, omit its symbol, and ignore an incomplete trailing codon.
- If the first edited codon is not an allowed initiator, use its ordinary internal codon meaning.

## Reference material

### Coding DNA to RNA

The supplied DNA is the coding strand, written 5′→3′. Copy its bases in the same order, replacing each T with U to obtain the coding RNA.

### Initiation of coding RNA

At the first RNA codon, UUG, CUG, AUU, AUC, AUA, AUG, and GUG encode initiating M under NCBI genetic code 11. If that first codon is not an allowed initiator, use its ordinary internal assignment.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal RNA codons

Each internal RNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
UUU=F  UUC=F  UUA=L  UUG=L

UAU=Y  UAC=Y  UAA=STOP  UAG=STOP

CCU=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAU=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGU=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
AUU=I  AUC=I  AUA=I  AUG=M
ACU=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAU=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGU=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GUU=V  GUC=V  GUA=V  GUG=V
GCU=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAU=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGU=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

## Task

Build the tagged CDS, translate it, apply ENLYFQ|G cleavage, and return the constructed DNA plus every resulting peptide and neutral monoisotopic mass.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGACTTTACTGACAGTATTGGGTTTCAAAACAGGTATAGTTTACATAGACGGACTTTAA",
  "tag_cds": "CACCACCATCACCACCACGAGAATTTATACTTTCAA",
  "linker_cds": "GGAGGGTCC",
  "placement": "N"
}
```

## Rules

- For N placement, assemble: original first codon + tag + linker + remaining original sense codons + original stop.
- For C placement, assemble: original sense codons + linker + tag + original stop.
- Translate with NCBI genetic code 11 and first-stop termination.
- Cut every exact `ENLYFQG` match as `ENLYFQ|G`; G stays on the downstream fragment.
- Calculate each unmodified fragment’s neutral monoisotopic mass independently, with free termini and one water per fragment.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Monoisotopic residue masses

Use the following incorporated residue masses in Da. A free linear peptide has the sum of its residue masses plus one H₂O, 18.010565 Da.

```text
A=71.037114  C=103.009185  D=115.026943  E=129.042593
F=147.068414  G=57.021464  H=137.058912  I=113.084064
K=128.094963  L=113.084064  M=131.040485  N=114.042927
P=97.052764  Q=128.058578  R=156.101111  S=87.032028
T=101.047679  V=99.068414  W=186.079313  Y=163.063329
```

## Answer

- `cds` is the exact constructed continuous uppercase DNA sequence. `fragments` lists every nonempty final TEV fragment in N-to-C order, retaining repeats.
- `peptide` is its continuous uppercase amino-acid sequence. `mass_da` is its neutral monoisotopic mass in Da as a JSON number, rounded once to 4 decimal places with one water per fragment.

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "cds": <DNA string>,
  "fragments": [
    {
      "peptide": <amino-acid string>,
      "mass_da": <number>
    }
  ]
}
```

## Task

Apply the DNA edits and report which complete tryptic peptide sequences are lost and gained relative to the original CDS.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

In an edit object, `replace` is `[first_base, last_base, new_text]`; an empty `new_text` deletes that interval. `insert_after` gives an original base boundary and `sequence` gives the inserted text. Apply all edits to the original coordinates, simultaneously.

```json
{
  "cds": "GTGTGGGGCGCCACCTTCCACAAGACGCTCTTCATGAGCGTGGTGCTCCGCATCGCCTACGTCATCTAG",
  "edits": [
    {
      "replace": [
        22,
        24,
        "GCA"
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 with first-stop termination.
- Apply the supplied edits simultaneously to the original DNA coordinates.
- Translate and completely digest both versions under `trypsin_KR_notP_v1`, with zero missed cleavages.
- Compare peptide sequence sets, ignoring copy number.

## Reference material

### Initiation after DNA editing

At the first DNA codon, TTG, CTG, ATT, ATC, ATA, ATG, and GTG encode initiating M under NCBI genetic code 11. If the first edited codon is not one of these initiators, use its ordinary internal assignment.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Trypsin cleavage specificity

Trypsin cleaves after K or R, except when the next residue is P. The K or R remains on the upstream peptide.

## Answer

- Apply the supplied edits simultaneously to the original DNA coordinates.

```text
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP

GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
```

```text
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Trypsin cleavage specificity

Trypsin cleaves after K or R, except when the next residue is P. The K or R remains on the upstream peptide.

## Answer

- `lost` lists distinct peptide sequences present in the original tryptic digest and absent from the edited digest. `gained` lists those present only in the edited digest.
- Sort each list in ASCII order. Each sequence appears once; an empty difference is [].

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "lost": [<amino-acid string>],
  "gained": [<amino-acid string>]
}
```

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Begin the raw JSON on the next line; it may span multiple lines and ends your response.

Final prediction

```json
{
  "lost": ["G", "RLDVAVK"],
  "gained": ["RLDVAVAG"]
}
```

## Task

Apply the DNA edits and report which complete tryptic peptide sequences are lost and gained relative to the original CDS.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

Coordinates and intervals are 1-based and inclusive unless a rule states otherwise.

In an edit object, `replace` is `[first_base, last_base, new_text]`; an empty `new_text` deletes that interval. `insert_after` gives an original base boundary and `sequence` gives the inserted text. Apply all edits to the original coordinates, simultaneously.

```json
{
  "cds": "GTGCGCACCGTAGAGGCCCTGGTGGAGGACCTGGGCTAG",
  "edits": [
    {
      "replace": [
        4,
        6,
        "GCA"
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

## Rules

- Use NCBI genetic code 11 with first-stop termination.
- Apply the supplied edits simultaneously to the original DNA coordinates.
- Translate and completely digest both versions under `trypsin_KR_notP_v1`, with zero missed cleavages.
- Compare peptide sequence sets, ignoring copy number.

## Reference material

### Initiation after DNA editing

At the first DNA codon, TTG, CTG, ATT, ATC, ATA, ATG, and GTG encode initiating M under NCBI genetic code 11. If the first edited codon is not one of these initiators, use its ordinary internal assignment.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Trypsin cleavage specificity

Trypsin cleaves after K or R, except when the next residue is P. The K or R remains on the upstream peptide.

## Answer

- `lost` lists distinct peptide sequences present in the original tryptic digest and absent from the edited digest. `gained` lists those present only in the edited digest.
- Sort each list in ASCII order. Each sequence appears once; an empty difference is [].

Use exactly this structure. `<…>` marks a typed value; array items and map entries repeat as needed.

### Output structure

```text
{
  "lost": [<amino-acid string>],
  "gained": [<amino-acid string>]
}
```

## Task

Calculate the encoded protein’s net charge at `ph`, using the independent-site Henderson–Hasselbalch model and supplied `pka` values.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCTCTACAACGGCGAGGTGAGCCTTTGCCCGGGCGCGATCACCTACGCGTAA",
  "ph": 8.0,
  "pka": {
    "N_term": 8.0,
    "C_term": 4.0,
    "K": 10.5,
    "R": 12.5,
    "H": 6.0,
    "D": 3.9,
    "E": 4.1,
    "C": 8.3,
    "Y": 10.1
  }
}
```

## Rules

- Translate the CDS using NCBI genetic code 11, stopping at the first in-frame stop.
- Use an unmodified linear protein with both termini free, and the supplied pKa for each site in the independent-site Henderson–Hasselbalch calculation.
- Keep the initiating residue. Assume no disulfide bonds or other modifications.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Ionization and net charge

A basic site contributes +1/(1+10^(pH−pKa)); an acidic site contributes −1/(1+10^(pKa−pH)).

Basic sites: the N terminus and K, R, H side chains. Acidic sites: the C terminus and D, E, C, Y side chains. Count each terminus once and each listed side chain once per residue occurrence; sum their contributions.

## Answer

- Return the net charge as a decimal number with 4 digits after the decimal point.
- Round once at the end using unrounded intermediate values and the declared constants. Round exact halfway cases away from zero; rounded negative zero becomes zero.

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

Expected answer

-1.8494

Final prediction

0.5001

- Digest every protein completely with trypsin, with zero missed cleavage sites.
- A peptide qualifies only if its sequence occurs in the target digest and in none of the comparison digests. Ignore how many copies occur.
- The peptide length must be between both inclusive `length_bounds`.

## Selection

From eligible target-only peptide sequences, choose the shortest, then the ASCII-lexicographically smallest sequence. Multiple copies of one sequence do not create separate choices.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Trypsin cleavage specificity

Trypsin cleaves after K or R, except when the next residue is P. The K or R remains on the upstream peptide.

## Answer

- Return one continuous uppercase one-letter amino-acid sequence, ending before the first in-frame stop.

Calculate the whole-protein mean Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy encoded by this CDS.

## Inputs

DNA inputs are 5′→3′ coding strands. Ignore whitespace between bases.

```json
{
  "cds": "ATGCAGCGCATCGACCGGATCGAGTTGAAATGCCGGGCGCTTGCCTCGGCACCGCTCTATTTTGGCTGA"
}
```

## Rules

- Translate the CDS with NCBI genetic code 11, stopping at the first in-frame stop.

## Reference material

### Annotated CDS initiation

The annotated first codon of an intact CDS encodes initiating methionine (M), even when its internal-codon assignment is different.

### NCBI genetic code 11: internal DNA codons

Each internal DNA codon is an aligned group of three bases. The table gives its one-letter amino-acid assignment; STOP marks a stop signal.

```text
TTT=F  TTC=F  TTA=L  TTG=L
TCT=S  TCC=S  TCA=S  TCG=S
TAT=Y  TAC=Y  TAA=STOP  TAG=STOP
TGT=C  TGC=C  TGA=STOP  TGG=W
CTT=L  CTC=L  CTA=L  CTG=L
CCT=P  CCC=P  CCA=P  CCG=P
CAT=H  CAC=H  CAA=Q  CAG=Q
CGT=R  CGC=R  CGA=R  CGG=R
ATT=I  ATC=I  ATA=I  ATG=M
ACT=T  ACC=T  ACA=T  ACG=T
AAT=N  AAC=N  AAA=K  AAG=K
AGT=S  AGC=S  AGA=R  AGG=R
GTT=V  GTC=V  GTA=V  GTG=V
GCT=A  GCC=A  GCA=A  GCG=A
GAT=D  GAC=D  GAA=E  GAG=E
GGT=G  GGC=G  GGA=G  GGG=G
```

### Kyte–Doolittle hydropathy scores

Use these Kyte–Doolittle residue scores. Whole-protein mean hydropathy is their arithmetic mean, counting every residue occurrence.

```text
A=1.8  R=-4.5  N=-3.5  D=-3.5
C=2.5  Q=-3.5  E=-3.5  G=-0.4
H=-3.2  I=4.5  L=3.8  K=-3.9
M=1.9  F=2.8  P=-1.6  S=-0.8
T=-0.7  W=-0.9  Y=-1.3  V=4.2
```

## Answer

- Return the mean hydropathy as a decimal number with 4 digits after the decimal point.
- Round once at the end using unrounded intermediate values and the declared constants. Round exact halfway cases away from zero; rounded negative zero becomes zero.

After any explanation, write `Final answer:` on its own line. Put the required value or list on the next line to finish your response.

Final prediction
